# Simulation catch-up investigation

The Steam shadow run on both PCs stayed within three estimated correction
frames with four frames of simulated input delay. It did not resimulate SC6.
The existing native replay experiments advance through natural updates;
`OfflineRollbackSession` has a synchronous adapter contract but no SC6 adapter.

## Current diagnostic

`tools/audit_update_entry.py` reads only the pinned executable. It produces a
disassembly, direct-call candidates confirmed at instruction boundaries, and
address-reference candidates. On the pinned executable it decoded 376
instructions at RVA 0x2DBC60 and found no direct callers or supported address
references. This is not proof that there are no callers: indirect dispatch,
tail jumps, and other reference encodings remain possible.

The entry bridge now passes the original caller's return address to the
recorder. An ordinary offline Training capture writes `entry_module_base`,
`entry_return_address`, and `entry_caller_changes` to its metadata. Addresses
are decimal. Resolve the return address against the loaded module containing
it before disassembling; do not assume it is in the main executable.

This diagnostic does not add simulation calls or change the game's return
address. The bridge test compares the observed address with the target's
actual return address and verifies mixed register/stack argument forwarding,
input replacement, and the returned value.

Next live step: install with SC6 closed, enter offline Training, and collect
one untouched ordinary 600-frame capture. Inspect the caller and its surrounding
work before implementing a bounded extra-update invocation. A callable ABI
alone does not establish deterministic catch-up or suppression of repeated
sound, rendering, networking, and other side effects.

## Confirmed caller capture and static follow-up

Capture `frames-54692-15340000.txt` completed 600 frames, zero skipped entries,
zero faults, zero counter discontinuities, and zero invalid input envelopes.
The user confirmed untouched controls, responsiveness, and no glitches.
All entries had the same return address, RVA `0x3FE816` in the main module.

The instruction preceding that address is an indirect virtual call at
`0x3FE813`, through `[vtable+0x68]`. The caller supplies an object from
`[rbx+0x1450]` in RCX and a stack input envelope in RDX. The observed battle
entry instead consumes the envelope in RCX. Therefore an intervening dispatch
adapter/tail-call path must be resolved before treating the virtual method
and battle entry as interchangeable callable functions.

The encompassing routine starts at `0x3FE520`; the captured unwind range was
only a chained fragment. It already contains a bounded iteration structure:

- Iteration count is computed at `0x3FE61D..0x3FE65B` using fields at
  owner+0x478, child+0x3A4, and owner+0x148C, with an alternate count-one path.
- Each iteration begins at `0x3FE6B0`. It builds an input envelope, calls
  `0x3FCD10` for each input slot, and assigns auxiliary data at owner+0x14C8.
- After the virtual update, it calls `0x3999C0`, `0x3FCE80`, and `0x3FCA60`.
- A separate branch at `0x3FE832..0x3FE842` clears owner+0x1462 and repeats
  from `0x3FE7F1`, without rebuilding the input envelope.
- The outer iteration decrements its counter and returns to `0x3FE6B0` at
  `0x3FE886..0x3FE891`.

These are control-flow observations, not identified rollback semantics. Do not
set the repeat flag or iteration count: input preparation and post-update work
have not been characterized for resimulation. In particular, directly invoking
the inner battle update would omit work performed by this surrounding loop.

Disassembly evidence is in `build-network/catch-up-caller.txt` and
`build-network/catch-up-surrounding.txt`. Next research should resolve the
virtual dispatch adapter and inspect `0x3FCD10` and the post-update routines,
including their chained unwind ranges, before adding a one-extra-update probe.
No live writes or extra simulation updates were performed in this follow-up.

## Dispatch adapter and helper audit

`tools/audit_catch_up.py` now follows chained unwind metadata to collect whole
functions rather than stopping at their first fragment. The pinned binary
produced 5 fragments/217 instructions for 0x3FE520, 5/88 for 0x3FCD10,
3/396 for 0x3FCE80, 8/190 for 0x3FCA60, and 5/54 for 0x3999C0.
Machine-readable evidence is `build-network/catch-up-audit.json`.

The missing dispatch adapter is at `0x3D2A20`: `mov rcx,rdx` followed by a
tail jump to `0x2DBC60`. Its absolute pointer is stored at RVA `0x327B678`,
consistent with slot +0x68 of a table at `0x327B610`. This explains the
captured return address and the input-envelope argument conversion. The tail
adapter has no unwind entry; its bytes and pointer reference support the
interpretation, rather than an assumed C++ signature.

Input preparation at 0x3FCD10 obtains frame-indexed input through 0x3F0720,
saves the prior input pair, updates the previous-held value, and computes
`(previous ^ current) & current` for new presses. It then applies suppression
flags and masks. This agrees with the earlier observed held/new-press pairs.
Replaying input acquisition itself would also change that history; catch-up
should use owned, recorded envelopes instead of polling live inputs again.

Post-update 0x3999C0 iterates a callback collection, invokes virtual callbacks,
and can tail-call a cleanup routine. 0x3FCE80 consumes a collection of state
codes at owner+0x1470 and changes owner+0x1480 with many conditional calls.
0x3FCA60 is gated on owner+0x528 and state code 2, then accesses additional
per-slot structures. Exact domain meanings are not established. These are
not proven pure simulation operations, so replaying the complete caller loop
would risk repeating callbacks and transitions.

Implementation direction: a bounded offline extra-update probe should call
the inner battle trampoline with an owned input envelope on the existing game
thread, avoiding recursive entry through the recorder. It must measure the
counter before/after and compare captured state against natural-update
references. First test one extra update; no online enablement follows from
this static audit alone. Side effects inside the battle function remain an
open question. The probe is not implemented or armed by this audit.

## One-update probe implementation

`NativeImmediateReplay.hpp` now implements a separately armed offline idle
experiment. It uses the existing guarded adjacent restore to capture A and B,
restores A, calls the original trampoline once with an owned A input envelope,
requires the outer counter to advance exactly once, and immediately invokes
the existing replay comparison against B. The original current update then
continues. It does not repeatedly call the outer callback/state-transition loop.

The caller RVA, neutral input fields, owner thread, snapshot guards, and counter
must pass. Invocation exceptions or comparison failures latch a failed recorder
session. There is no retry or online trigger. Metadata reports actual invocation,
counter values, exception code, restoration/comparison results, and failure stage.
This is a bounded experiment, not a complete snapshot or proven catch-up adapter.

After installation and a fresh launch in unpaused offline Training, use
`scripts/Arm-SingleUpdate.ps1 -TrainingConfirmed`. Leave controls untouched.
Restart the game afterward, including after a failed comparison. The launcher
requires a matching installed build, no pending arm, and no previous native or
Steam-input experiment from this process. No live result exists yet.

### One-frame live result and three-frame extension

`native-adjacent-idle-17372-16283609.txt` passed: one synchronous invocation,
counter 878 to 879, restoration and reference comparison verified, 600
continuous samples, and no reported faults or exceptions. The user confirmed
untouched controls and normal responsiveness without glitches.

`NativeThreeImmediateReplay.hpp` extends the experiment without changing the
one-frame mode. It captures four natural idle boundaries, prepares all three
adjacent restore guards before writes, restores backwards through the three
boundaries, and then invokes three updates synchronously. Every update uses
its own recorded input envelope and one-shot invocation guard; every resulting
frame must pass the existing comparison before the next update is attempted.
Failure stops the experiment and requires a restart. The normal current update
still runs after the probe returns. Use `Arm-ThreeUpdates.ps1 -TrainingConfirmed`
only after installing the new build and launching fresh offline Training.
Three-frame live validation is pending; online rollback remains disabled.

### Three-frame first attempt

`native-adjacent-idle-56956-16509750.txt` captured four references and passed
all three preparation and restoration checks. The first synchronous update
advanced the counter 783 to 784 without exception, but its reference comparison
failed; the remaining two calls were not attempted. The user confirmed normal
responsiveness with no visible glitches, then closed SC6.

The follow-up build adds per-hop comparison diagnostics: measured RNG/player
identity and scalar byte differences, replay-save completion/fault, keys,
animation buffers, native payload, blend, transform and camera equality. When
a replay save exists it exports expected/actual payloads and record layouts
for offline byte attribution. These diagnostics do not relax any comparison
or change the three-call limit. The cause of the mismatch is not yet known.

### Native difference attribution

`native-adjacent-idle-57740-16911468` reproduced the first-replay mismatch.
RNG, player identities, scalar bytes, keys, rings, blends, transforms and cameras
all matched. Native expected and actual payload lengths were both 106766 bytes.
Of 13 raw differing bytes, 12 belonged to the previously handled player
self-pointer records (writer RVA 0x30AA54). The additional difference was in
record 349, writer RVA 0x30A9CE, source RVA 0x47ACB70, length 13584, offset 6828:
expected 0, actual 1. The capture's player pointer maps this to P2+0x1B3C
(main-module RVA 0x47AE61C).

Static tracing establishes that this is part of a 16-bit field, not padding.
The serializer writes the player+0x90 block of length 0x3510, which includes it.
Routine 0x304D50 selects fields from player+0x1B38 using a two-byte index.
Index 2 selects +0x1B3C. It adds a supplied delta, clamps that field to 0..240,
and dispatches a virtual notification when the resulting value changes.
Other direct accesses initialize or read this field; its player-facing name
has not been established.

One relevant caller is 0x3425A0: it reads a supplied record's type, mode, and
value at +4/+8/+12, and record type 4 calls 0x304D50 with index 2 at 0x3426F7.
It releases the supplied record via 0x2DD8F0 afterward. This is a candidate
source of the replay-only increment, not a confirmed runtime attribution.
Another call path at 0x3793D7 accepts a variable field index and value.

Evidence: `build-network/player-1b3c-references.txt` and
`build-network/player-field-update-callers.txt`. Stack-offset coincidences in
unrelated functions are not player-field accesses. No mismatch was ignored,
no compensation write was added, and no live experiment ran during this audit.
Next diagnostic should attribute writes/updates of field index 2 during the
bounded replay, including caller and delta, to distinguish an extra queued
operation from another state dependency. Preserve the exact native comparator.

### Bounded field-update trace

The three-frame mode now enables an additional pinned-prologue hook at
0x304D50. `FieldUpdateBridge.asm` preserves the volatile registers, floating
point environment, arguments, caller return address and original return path.
It invokes a read-only trace callback before tail-jumping to the trampoline.
No function-signature return type is assumed and the original call runs once.

`FieldUpdateTrace.hpp` captures at most 256 calls for field index 2 from the
probe's owner thread. It records caller, player, counter, requested signed
16-bit delta, prior 16-bit value, flags and phase. Phase 1 is reference capture
and natural playback, 2 restoration, 3 synchronous replay, 4 comparison.
This observes calls to one update routine, not every possible writer to the
field. Unobserved direct writes remain possible. It does not establish that
the requested delta was actually applied; the routine can reject or clamp it.

The trace stops on completion/failure/timeout and is serialized as
`*-field-updates.csv`. The hook remains pass-through afterward. A unit test
verifies original argument/return preservation, filtering, bounded capture,
stop behavior, LastError preservation and absence of field writes. Installation
does not arm the experiment; fresh offline Training confirmation is required.

### Increment trace result and assignment follow-up

Capture `native-adjacent-idle-54936-17711328` completed all three immediate
updates, but only the first two comparisons passed. The third differed at
the same P2+0x1B3C value (expected zero, actual one). The trace observed
identical index-2, delta-1 requests from return RVA 0x3793DC at counter 1098
in both the natural and replay phases; both saw a prior value of zero.
No trace entries were dropped. This does not support a duplicate update call.
The user confirmed responsiveness without glitches.

Static follow-up identified assignment routine 0x2D6D70, which writes the
signed nonnegative word from its second argument+0xC to player+0x1B3C.
Caller 0x2D73C0 invokes it for both players at 0x2D73FC and 0x2D740C.
This may explain a normal-path reset, but runtime attribution remains pending.
Other initialization/reset writers exist, so this is not an exhaustive trace.

The next diagnostic adds a read-only entry trace at 0x2D6D70 using the same
register-preserving bridge pattern. The CSV now uses `requested_value` and
`assignment`: assignment 0 means an additive request; 1 means a nonnegative
direct-assignment request. Negative settings values are skipped just as in
the native routine. Tests cover zero assignments, negative sentinel values,
invalid settings pointers, and absence of writes. No corrective replay changes
or comparison exceptions have been introduced.

It captured 15 additive requests for P2 index 2, requested_value=1 and before=0, spaced exactly 40 counters apart (1177 through 1737), caller RVA 0x3793DC. No assignment-entry events or dropped events. Every recorded boundary retained P1=240 and P2=0. ### Post-call observation prepared
Added a read-only hook at pinned RVA 0x3793DC (the jump immediately after the observed increment call). It preserves volatile registers/flags and observes the player via preserved RDI; assignment=2 denotes a post-call observation, with the CSV before column containing the value at that observation. Unlike increment-entry records, these observations are emitted for every visit to this call site and must be correlated with the preceding index-2 entry record, not assumed to independently prove an index-2 operation. A dedicated mid-function harness checks stack alignment, player argument, continuation, and return value preservation. Recorder tests passed. Installation requires closing the running game.

### Post-call capture result
native-adjacent-idle-49756-19638640: 600 continuous frames, fault=0, 28 events, none dropped; no restoration or extra updates. ### Setter identified
native-adjacent-idle-55232-21935546: 600 continuous frames, fault=0, setter_fault=0, 90 field events. Setter inventory reached 16 distinct temporary objects (overflow=1); inventory is not exhaustive. All retained setter entries use value=0 at 0x428334 and target RVA 0x3CD170. This thunk adds 8 to RCX and tail-jumps to 0x2D3B10. Disassembly proves 0x2D3B10 reads player index at adjusted receiver+8 (original object+16), resolves player via pointer array RVA 0x470DE90, checks player+0x43E2C==0, caps EDX at 0x7FFF, then writes DX to player+0x1B3C at RVA 0x2D3B32. The low DWORD of retained word2 is 1, consistent with P2. Thus observed zero setter path directly writes the suspect field, gated by an existing flag. The correction must preserve these semantics and applicable Training mode rather than unconditionally zeroing the field or replaying the full dispatcher. Saved disassembly in build-network/resolved-setter.txt. Visual confirmation pending; no restoration or extra updates performed.

### Guarded offline Training correction
User confirmed setter capture normal/untouched. Added TrainingFieldReset: observes the real setter call per player on each normal reference interval, recording player pointer, counter, requested 0/240 and player+0x43E2C guard. Before applying a recorded setter effect, validates counter, pointer-table identity, allowed value and unchanged guard for all samples; requires a P2 sample. Writes the 16-bit field only when the recorded/current native guard is zero. This reproduces the verified setter semantics for this offline probe without replaying the callback dispatcher. It is not a production/network correction or proof of complete outer-loop coverage.
Three-frame immediate probe now waits near the previously observed 40-update increment interval so its window can include the mismatch; output reports waited_for_increment and training_resets_applied. References retain their exact native/scalar/animation/camera comparisons. Test evidence must include an actual increment in the captured/replayed window; a comparison-only pass without that event is insufficient. Added unit checks for zero/240 application, stale-counter rejection, changed-guard rejection and nonzero guard no-write behavior. Installation pending game closure.

### Peer12196 pipeline analysis

## Auxiliary ownership trace and input composer (2026-10-09)
Hash-pinned static audit saved to build-network/auxiliary-flow-pinned.json; reproducible with tools/audit_auxiliary_flow.py. Follows chained unwind fragments.
- The outer loop calls3FE960 before iterating battle updates. It selects a source index from session/controller metadata, reads owner+450 source+3E0 with144-byte stride into owner+14C8, and computes owner+14CC=(old XOR new) AND new. Four values from owner+440 at offsets42C,430,424,428 (168-byte stride) populate14D0..14DC.
- At3FE71B the envelope's third pointer is owner+14C8; first two pointers are separate player pairs populated through3FCD10. The latter obtains processed player input through3F0720 and computes rising edges with additional masks.
- Battle entry2DBC60 copies auxiliary words5/6 to globals470D100/104 and words7..10 to470D110..11C. Direct references found include37D95D,37DA94,37DA9B,37DB09, where auxiliary data reaches camera-related object control. This is not exhaustive proof of all indirect consumers or proof that auxiliaries are irrelevant to gameplay.
Policy for the candidate adapter: retain this PC's historical auxiliary block, replace only the assigned remote player's processed held/edge pair. Never wholesale copy the remote envelope or recompute auxiliary flags from combat inputs.
Added standalone NetworkInputComposer.hpp with no hooks/memory writes. Test-NetworkInputComposer.ps1 passed both slots, exact releases, preserving all other words, rejecting unequal counters/invalid slots, and immutable source checks; added it to Build-Native's test list. ## Ping estimate revision (2026-10-09)
User requested pure network ping excluding processing. The active SteamNetworking005 GetP2PSessionState path exposes connection/queue/relay info but no RTT; modern SteamNetworkingSockets RTT requires its connection handle and cannot simply query this legacy connection. Reference:https://partner.steamgames.com/doc/api/ISteamNetworking . Exact wire RTT cannot be established by the current polling echo.
Changed SteamPing to five probes spaced at least20ms per3-second burst, selecting the minimum valid RTT after subtracting only responder-measured receive-to-reply preparation duration. Burst closes after all five replies or500ms; partial bursts accepted, all-lost bursts retain prior reading only until9-second expiry. Receive timestamp taken immediately after Steam ReadP2PPacket, before validation/diagnostic processing. Unknown Steam/OS queueing and receive-poll waits remain. No assumed frame-duration subtraction or cross-PC absolute clock comparison. UI label PING ~ communicates estimate; report retains raw selected microseconds, removed handling duration, sample count and ping_network_only=0. This estimates low-queue RTT rather than typical/jitter latency and cannot promise a lower measured value in every session.
New input handshake signature000753433648534B requires both peers on this build. Ping packets carry timing marker1 and64-bit responder duration. Wrong IDs, duplicates, replies outside500ms window, backwards timestamps, impossible duration, and failed sends are rejected. Focused tests passed. Pinned executable SHA256 F8904E4B04BCA3B47BC52A683F6190365D2EB89EE8F44F8072759E9C5E04A553. Static research only; no DLL or live game changes.

Global470E038 is initialized from module storage at2DA310/2DA317, but its linked nodes are dynamically allocated. The result receives vtable3E88CE8, manager at+28, next at+10, previous at+18. Instructions334A6B..334A7E prepend it and update the previous head's back link. Its0x1E0 allocation agrees with captured24-byte header plus0x1C8 kind24 payload. Another insertion at2FC767..2FC796 uses vtable3E88D18 and the same link layout. These are concrete creation/list-change paths, not evidence that old object addresses remain valid.

Do not relax same_topology or restore stale list pointers based on these findings. Removal/free paths and native serializer ownership remain unproven; changed-topology restoration needs an explicit lifetime strategy.334750 is RNG/effect initialization, not a demonstrated list-clear function;2D9F30 walks another source list and constructs effect-related entries, not a proven deallocator.

Evidence: work/dynamic-manager-references.txt, dynamic-node-construction.txt, dynamic-lifecycle-followup.txt, dynamic-lifecycle-clear.txt. ### Confirmed dynamic-effect unlink and allocator release path
Pinned executable investigation located the manager update at333FD0. At334066 it loads manager head;334080..3340B5 invokes node virtual+18 (initialized), optionally+8 (initialization), then+10 (update), and+20 (alive). For3E88CE8, getters331070/331080 read+68/+6C. A zero alive result takes3340B7..3340F4: replace vtable with base3E88D48, splice previous+10 and next+18, replace manager head when removing its first node, then call1F90000 with the node address. The saved next pointer in RDI advances traversal after removal.

1F90000 jumps toD46A00. That dispatcher uses allocator global41971C8 and virtual+20 with the node pointer, or fallbackD46FD0. Allocation D4F760 uses the SAME global41971C8 and virtual+10 with requested byte size. Thus node removal hands memory back to the allocator; a saved address is not a lifetime guarantee. EDx=70 at the removal call is overwritten by the allocator dispatcher and must not be treated as the concrete node allocation size (CE8 allocation is1E0).

Evidence: work/dynamic-unlink-scan.txt (candidate search); work/dynamic-unlink-confirmed.txt (334066 onward; preceding334060 starts mid-instruction and is excluded); work/dynamic-release-dispatch.txt. Calls/branches were checked against the instruction-aligned333FD0 unwind fragment. No runtime mutations or production code changes.

Implementation consequence: retain same_topology rejection until lifetime handling exists. A possible bounded ownership journal would intercept ONLY verified effect creation/release sites, defer allocator release while a rollback snapshot references a node, retain original allocation size/type/generation, and quarantine speculative creations until history commits. Restore must preflight the complete manager list and generations before reconstructing links, and retirement must eventually invoke the original allocator exactly once. This is a design candidate, not implemented support: remaining work includes enumerating other release/reset sites and external aliases, checking native load interactions, and defining cleanup on round/session changes. A general allocator hook or restoring freed addresses is not justified by this evidence.

### Effect cleanup call-site audit and retention boundary
Extended pinned-image scan to direct CALL and tail JMP targets333FD0 (effect update),1F90000 andD46A00 (release). Candidates were accepted only when decoding from their containing exception-table fragment reached the instruction boundary. In game RVA range2D0000..350000 the audit found89 transfers total, including two paths to the effect update: battle call2DC336 and tail dispatch2DCE59 from routine2DCB30. Both reach the same expired-node unlink/release at3340F4. In local effect range330000..335000,3340F4 was the only direct transfer found to these release targets. This is a scoped static inventory, NOT exhaustive proof against indirect calls, leaf functions absent from exception metadata, or other allocator entry points.

The alternate2DCB30 path also calls generator334960 at2DCE35 before updating effects, just as battle2DBC60 calls it at2DC325. Thus ownership tracking limited to the main battle-entry hook would miss an independently reachable creation/update path. The purpose/callers of2DCB30 need tracing; do not label it a serializer merely from its proximity to restoration routines. Its global nonnull check at2DCE46 targets470E038.

Retention design refinement: an interception at release3340F4 occurs AFTER unlinking and after overwriting the node vtable with base3E88D48. Deferring the free alone does not preserve an intact object or its list membership. A snapshot must retain original type/full supported payload and ownership links; restore must validate allocation generation before reconstructing them. A narrowly gated interception inside the common manager routine covers both known update entry paths, whereas only wrapping the battle entry does not. Unknown release paths must remain a blocker to pointer resurrection; no topology guard was relaxed.

Artifacts: work/effect-cleanup-audit.json (89 verified direct transfers), work/effect-cleanup-tail.txt, work/effect-loader-update.txt (name is exploratory, not a proven loader identification), work/effect-base-vtable-references.txt. No production changes, compilation, installation, or live capture in this research step. Next actionable investigation: callers and serializer relationship of2DCB30, plus session teardown ownership; then a bounded lifetime tracker with unit tests before enabling any interception.

### Bounded effect lifetime ledger (not connected to native code)
Added EffectLifetimeLedger.hpp and Test-EffectLifetime.ps1. Fixed-capacity single-thread bookkeeping stores verified allocation address/type/size, monotonically increasing allocation generation, snapshot-slot pins, and retired/live state. Retired pinned allocations cannot be collected. Restored objects can be marked live only with an existing snapshot pin; this does not perform or authorize payload/list restoration. Collection invalidates the token and calls a noexcept release callback once after the last pin expires. Live allocations are never collected merely because history expires. Duplicate addresses and capacity/invalid-argument failures reject without evicting retained objects. No general clear/reset discards ownership. Future owner must release a snapshot slot before reusing it and must provide verified creation/removal interception plus shutdown handling.

Targeted MSVC /W4 /WX test passed: multi-snapshot retention, revive/retire, exact-once release, stale tokens after address reuse, bounded exhaustion, duplicate pins, invalid indices/allocations, and64-slot bit boundary. Added test to Build-Native; full DLL rebuild not needed for this unreferenced standalone header. No game hooks, writes, installation or new staged DLL.

Its body demonstrably calls the generator and tail-dispatches the manager, but actual runtime reachability is NOT established. Could be unused code or reached through an unrecognized mechanism. Do not assert the active game uses a second path. Session teardown and indirect aliases remain unresolved; native retention must stay disabled until that coverage is established.

### Generated stage buffer cleanup contract

Traced the control vtable at RVA 0x3E89308. Strong disposal (0x37C7B0) passes control+0x18 to the callback at control+0x10; callback 0x37C620 jumps to the CRT free import at 0x322D2F0. Weak disposal (0x2DE1D0) invokes the deleting-destructor slot with flag 1. That implementation (0x348F50) releases the 32-byte control block through 0x1F90000. Combined with the previously traced atomic decrements, this establishes the observed generated-buffer disposal path, not a safe acquisition or synchronization contract.

### Descriptor setter found outside unwind metadata

Corrected a research-search blind spot: the previous RIP-relative inventory only decoded instructions inside exception-directory functions, excluding leaf routines. Expanded raw candidate scanning found the actual setter at 0x2DAE70, bounded by padding and RET, which copies source+0 and source+8 into globals 0x470D0D0 and 0x470D0F8. The full routine also copies other pointers and two flags. No allocation, reference acquisition or release occurs in this setter. Its direct tail-call forwarder at 0x3CFC20 moves RDX to RCX then jumps to the setter. The actual caller supplying that structure and its ownership remain unresolved.

Raw candidate results contain overlapping instruction decodes and must not be treated as verified instruction boundaries. The setter and forwarder were separately decoded from their padded starts and their exact bytes were added to the pinned executable audit, which passed. Repro scripts: work/find_stage_descriptor_leaf_refs.py, inspect_descriptor_setter.py, find_descriptor_setter_callers.py, inspect_descriptor_forwarder.py. No DLL changes, installation or live test. ### Descriptor dispatch interface identified

The forwarder 0x3CFC20 is referenced by table entry 0x327B650. Constructor references at 0x3DCC60 and 0x441396 establish the actual interface vtable base as 0x327B610, making the setter slot +0x40. Do not infer a vtable start by scanning backward over adjacent executable pointers: adjacent tables here have no non-code separator; that heuristic produced an incorrect +0x1B08 candidate and no callers. Exact constructor LEAs and the +0x40 target now pass the pinned executable audit.

Construction allocates a small interface object and a separate reference-counted wrapper. In constructor 0x441340 the interface pair is placed at object+0x3B0/+0x3B8, followed by call 0x39AE30. This owns the forwarding interface, not proven ownership of the descriptor argument. The slot forwards its second argument to the global setter without acquiring descriptor references. Next trace virtual +0x40 invocations through this interface and the supplied argument's producer; descriptor release/lifetime still unresolved. No runtime/DLL changes or live capture.

Evidence: work/descriptor-dispatch-pointers.txt, descriptor-table-xrefs.json, descriptor-interface-construction.txt and their corresponding inspection scripts.

### Descriptor producer and resource-list synchronization

Located the actual interface +0x40 call at 0x3FA4E9 through object+0x1450. Its stack argument is zero-initialized by 0x2DCF90 and populated by 0x420D80 -> 0x4208B0. The latter selects entries from the list returned by 0x89D3A0, maps names through a separate metadata table, and copies entry+0x10 into output+0/+8 for the two descriptors. Temporary list entries are 32 bytes; their strings and container are freed afterward, not the selected payload pointers.

0x89D3A0 guards enumeration with the critical section at object+0x208 (TryEnterCriticalSection, fallback EnterCriticalSection, then LeaveCriticalSection). The source container begins at object+0x100. Each 32-byte source entry supplies a resource object at +8 and control pointer at +16; two temporary strong-reference increments at 0x89D5EB/0x89D5F0 are paired with release paths. Resource virtual slot 0 returns the raw payload, stored into output entry+0x10 at 0x89D830. Returned list does not carry those temporary resource references. This identifies an ownership acquisition location, but does not prove payload lifetime after enumeration or the concrete resource destructor. Do not activate retention on this evidence alone.

Pinned audit extended with producer calls, pointer transfers and reference-acquisition bytes; passed. Evidence: work/descriptor-virtual-call-candidates.txt, descriptor-producer.txt, descriptor-source-fields.txt, descriptor-resource-list.txt and inspection scripts. No DLL change or live test. Next: identify resource implementations inserted into this container, their slot-0 payload accessor and final destruction; verify lock covers replacement/removal too.

### Resource container clear uses the enumeration lock

Found container-clear function 0x899110: acquires owner+0x208 via TryEnterCriticalSection/EnterCriticalSection, invokes 0x1C14E80 on owner+0x100, clears hash state and the secondary container, then tail-jumps to LeaveCriticalSection. 0x1C14E80 iterates entries, reads control pointer at entry+0x10, decrements strong count at control+8, invokes control slot 0 on last strong reference, then decrements weak count at +0xC and invokes slot 8 on last weak reference. Thus this clearing path releases resource references under the same lock used by enumeration; it does not unconditionally free raw descriptor pointers. Exact sites added to pinned audit and passed.

This supports retaining resource objects under the enumeration lock to survive this particular clear path, but does not establish the concrete resource's payload ownership, in-place mutation behavior, all removal paths, or owner-object lifetime. No reference counts were changed. Resource implementations/destructors and insertion paths still require tracing before retention can be activated.

Other inspected paths 0x89EAA0/0x89EDxx iterate slot +0x10 predicates and update readiness flags; do not label them destructor/removal paths just because they take the same lock. 0x8A1D20 includes container mutation and asynchronous-work setup, requiring further analysis. Evidence: work/resource-owner-lifecycle.txt, resource-container-clear.txt, resource-removal.txt and reproducible inspection scripts. No DLL changes, installation, or live test.

### Population lock and asynchronous readiness separated

Traced 0x8A1D20: RDX is the supplied primary collection, R8 the secondary collection, RCX the owner. The function acquires owner+0x208 using the same critical-section imports, checks existing collections, and assigns a prepared collection into owner+0x100 via 0x863A60 at 0x8A1E5F before releasing the lock at 0x8A24F8. Direct-call inventory identified 13 candidate caller sites (work/resource-container-population-callers.txt), including several stage setup paths. This is additional evidence that publication is synchronized, not proof that every mutation is covered.

Followed the callback installed at 0x89F48F to its correctly calculated target 0x898230. It tests +0x1A1, dispatches completion state via 0x897E70, then sets +0x1A0. The separately inspected 0x897230 address was an arithmetic false lead; resource-load-completion.txt is not evidence for this callback. Readiness completion is not a payload lifetime guarantee. Concrete resource construction, payload accessor implementation and destructor remain unresolved; retention has not been activated.

Added pinned instruction/call checks for population locking, assignment and readiness callback, and the executable audit passed. No DLL changes or game test. Next follow the primary collection supplied by the stage setup callers to find concrete resource/control-block types, avoiding unrelated async delegate types in the loader.

### Concrete resource owns the exposed payload

Followed collection builder 0x2DDBFB0 -> 0x2DDC010 -> factory 0x2DDB8C0. Factory may return an existing pair from 0x2DDE1C0; on creation it allocates 0xC0 bytes and invokes 0x2DD9BB0, setting resource vtable 0x3CEA0F8. Its 24-byte control block uses vtable 0x36E9D40. Resource slot 0 (0x2DDDA30) checks readiness via slot +0x10, then returns resource+0x38; slot +8 returns the size field +0x48. Control strong disposal 0x29B9940 dispatches resource deleting-destructor slot +0x38, target 0x2DDB220. This calls 0x2DDA4E0 -> base cleanup 0x2DDA440; base cleanup reads the identical payload pointer at +0x38 and frees it via 0xD46A00, then cleans up other fields. Thus the newly created concrete resource owns the payload returned to enumeration.

Strong retention prevents this destructor-triggered disposal, but does not yet prove that no live method can replace/free +0x38 independently. Existing cached objects must also be type-checked; this is not proof every factory return has this implementation. Next audit payload mutation/reset paths and insertion helper reference acquisition before implementing guarded retention. No native changes or new capture.

Pinned audit extended with factory/constructor/vtable/accessor/destructor-chain checks and passed. Repro evidence: work/resource-entry-factory.txt, resource-payload-constructor.txt, resource-payload-methods.txt, resource-payload-destructor.txt, resource-payload-cleanup.txt, resource-payload-base-cleanup.txt and their inspection scripts.

### Strong retention is insufficient during payload loading

Found non-destructor payload mutation in loader 0x2DDBB10 (including chained fragment 0x2DDBEC9). It passes resource+0x38 to growth helper 0x1F7DC10 and shrink helper 0x11E1B10, then clears the payload through 0x311C070 using the pointer at +0x38 and size +0x48. Caller 0x2DDACE0 promotes a weak reference using atomic compare/exchange, invokes the loader at 0x2DDADA7, then passes the resulting payload into an I/O virtual call. The resource can therefore be strongly referenced while its backing buffer is changing. Need a verified completed-loading/no-reload contract in addition to lifetime retention; readiness alone has not yet been proven sufficient.

Exact loader calls and pointer operands added to pinned audit; passed using the correct Win64 executable path after an initial command used an incorrect parent path. No game modification. Candidate scans are scoped to nearby functions and are not exhaustive mutation coverage. resource-all-methods.txt includes adjacent table entries beyond the known destructor slot; do not assume those entries belong to this resource type. Evidence: work/resource-payload-loader.txt, resource-payload-loader-callers.txt, resource-payload-address-candidates.txt and reproducible scripts. Next trace when +0x50 becomes ready and whether this same object can re-enter 0x2DDACE0 afterward.

### Loading readiness and scheduling path

Concrete constructor sets resource+0x50 false at 0x2DD9BCA. Completion callback 0x2DDA9E0 promotes a weak control reference, sets +0x50 true at 0x2DDAA33, then may invoke the resource's completion delegate. Scheduler 0x2DE1670 constructs two weak-bound callbacks: wrapper 0x2DDC800 -> completion 0x2DDA9E0 and wrapper 0x2DDC850 -> loader 0x2DDACE0. Passes the pair to 0xE69090 and stores the returned task handle at resource+0x58/+0x60. Task ordering/one-shot guarantees inside 0xE69090 are not yet proven.

Direct E8/E9 scan found one candidate scheduler caller, 0x2DDB9FD, verified on the factory's newly allocated resource path. Cache-hit path returns before scheduling. No same-object requeue found in that path; this is not exhaustive proof against indirect calls or task retries. The ready flag is written before its optional completion delegate, so it alone is not a synchronization barrier for all completion work. Next inspect the task scheduler's completion ordering/retry contract and cache eviction versus resource mutation.

Exact constructor/ready writes, wrapper jumps, callback references and scheduler calls added to pinned audit; passed. No runtime changes or reinstall. Evidence work/resource-ready-completion.txt, resource-load-scheduling.txt, resource-load-schedule-callers.txt. The generic wrapper dump continued after leaf jumps into adjacent functions; only the first jump at each wrapper is relevant.

### Loader worker ordering and completion caveat

Scheduler mode 2, explicitly passed by 0x2DE1670, creates worker vtable 0x35319C0. Worker slot 0 is 0xE71380: invokes stored loader callback at worker+0x10, stores its return in shared task state+0x60, calls completion dispatcher 0xE771C0, then invokes its own deleting destructor. No retry/back-edge occurs in this worker body. Dispatcher sets task state+0x58 true and signals its event before invoking the completion callback. Therefore task done/event cannot alone establish that the completion callback has returned. Resource ready+0x50 is likewise set before its optional delegate. Need to distinguish payload mutation completion from completion-delegate cleanup; neither flag is automatically a full quiescence barrier.

Inspected 0x2DDFB20 previously labeled a cache-removal candidate: it marks matching entries in pending groups and compacts completed groups. Do not treat it as proven resource-cache eviction; payload cache lifetime still requires tracing separately. This step proves local worker ordering, not global absence of requeue or indirect loader calls. Exact worker vtable/callback/completion/deletion sites added to executable audit, which passed. No native retention or DLL changes.

Evidence: work/resource-task-runner.txt, resource-task-complete-dispatch.txt, resource-task-cache-contract.txt and corresponding inspection scripts. Next inspect the completion delegate's effects on payload and the actual cache lookup/insertion/eviction paths.

### Completion delegate and weak cache ownership

Factory completion delegate address from 0x2DDB953 resolves to 0x2DDC840: loads the captured manager pointer and tail-jumps to 0x2DDFB20, the previously inspected pending-group bookkeeping routine. That routine marks completion by key and compacts pending groups; no direct payload +0x38 mutation was found in the inspected body. Called delegates remain a transitive dependency; do not claim full absence of side effects.

Actual cache lookup 0x2DDE1C0 uses 40-byte map entries with object/control at +0x10/+0x18, checks strong count, and atomically promotes the weak reference using compare/exchange at 0x2DDE236. Factory inserts via 0x2DE0C80; this increments weak count +0xC, invokes map insertion 0x2DD8C80, and releases temporary weak and incoming strong references. Cache presence is therefore not payload retention. This narrows the plan to explicit strong retention of a verified ready resource, plus loader exclusion/quiescence and descriptor identity checks. Actual cache erasure/replacement helper internals still not fully audited.

Added exact delegate jump, cache insertion, weak increment and lookup promotion checks to audit_effect_lifetime.py. Pinned audit passed. No DLL changes or live tests; continuous rollback and native retention remain disabled. Evidence: work/resource-cache-lookup-delegate.txt, resource-cache-insert.txt and scripts. Next inspect map insertion/replacement weak-reference cleanup and formulate a bounded runtime identity/readiness observer before enabling any reference modification.

### Cache replacement verified; runtime observation contract

0x2DD8C80 allocates/inserts an entry and checks for an existing key. Replacement reads the old control at entry+0x18, decrements only its weak count (+0xC), calls control slot +8 on final weak release, frees the old key string, then transfers 32 bytes from the new entry and removes the redundant slot. It does not invoke strong disposal or directly free the resource payload. Added exact weak-release and replacement sites to pinned audit; passed. Full arbitrary cache erasure and indirect mutations remain outside this assertion.

Concrete next observer design (not implemented or installed): observe the resource accessor during 0x89D3A0 enumeration, where native code already holds temporary strong references and the container lock. Filter to the verified resource vtable and exact caller before sparse reads. Record bounded object identity, native control identity if captured at the enumeration callsite, payload +0x38, size +0x48, ready +0x50 and task-handle identity; do not interpret task done as completion quiescence. Match payload addresses against the descriptor globals/root at a later native save boundary, using identity records rather than dereferencing released resource objects. Preserve native result, LastError, and exception behavior; no reference modification, no calling loaders or save/restore, no file I/O or blocking waits inside the native hook. Bounded event buffer with explicit dropped/fault counters; reject unknown types and incomplete matches. Pointer equality alone is not generation identity, and observations cannot establish ownership or authorize retention automatically.

Hook selection still requires a verified prologue and calling contract; hooking the accessor alone does not expose the control pointer, so either capture it from a validated native enumeration boundary or leave control identity unavailable. Do not guess it from adjacent allocation memory. Need meaningful fixture tests for filtering, capacity, stale captures, and return/LastError preservation before a DLL build. One live capture should establish the concrete resource-to-descriptor mapping before any retention code is activated.

Evidence: work/resource-cache-replacement.txt and inspection script. No DLL modification, installation or live test this step.

### Read-only resource descriptor observer implemented

`ResourceDescriptorObserver.hpp` hooks the pinned payload accessor at RVA 0x2DDDA30 and records only return caller 0x89D79C. FrameRecorder polls it only after executable hash verification. The hook performs bounded metadata reads while enumeration retains the object; it does not retain resources, dereference control pointers, allocate, write files, or restore state. Publication uses a try-lock and arm epoch. Unknown objects and read faults produce invalid records. Capacity is 4096 events; drops are reported.

Arm: write `RESOURCE_DESCRIPTORS_ONLY <game PID>` to `captures/resource-descriptors.arm`. After the ready report, load a stage within the 120-second window. Reports are `resource-descriptors-<PID>-<tick>.txt/.csv`. Descriptor equality is sampled at accessor return and can be absent if the setter runs afterward; absence is not proof that the resource is unrelated. This establishes neither allocation lifetime nor reload safety. Continuous rollback remains disabled.

Fixture coverage: valid object/descriptor metadata, caller filtering, faulting pointer, capacity overflow, return value and LastError preservation. Live observation remains pending.

### Delayed reference lease helper and disposal audit

Implemented ResourceReferenceLease.hpp, not connected to production hooks. It acquires only from an already-owned pair, is noncopyable, enforces an explicitly supplied release thread and three-frame minimum, and supports explicit cancellation. On last strong release it calls the supplied object disposer, then drops the implicit weak reference and calls the control disposer only on last weak. It clears its active handle before callbacks to prevent double release. Callers must explicitly drain leases; no arbitrary-thread RAII destruction is provided. Count integrity and pointer validity remain integration preconditions.

Fixture tests passed: three-frame delay, duplicate acquire/release, original-owner retirement, last-strong/last-weak and remaining-weak paths, wrong-thread rejection, cancellation, dead-count rejection. These are synthetic frame numbers, not an in-game cross-frame test.

Pinned audit now verifies resource control slot1 -> RVA 0x216D8C0, deleting flag in EDX and 24-byte control deallocation via 0x1F90000; strong disposer 0x29B9940 forwards to resource deleting slot 0x38 with flag1. Audit passed. Production wrapper must supply flag1 for control disposal; never call its two-argument native ABI as the helper's one-argument callback directly. ResourceReferenceLease is not wired into a live capture: game-thread boundary selection, draining/exit handling and cancellation integration remain required. Installed DLL unchanged; no game restart required now.

### Game-boundary lease controller

Implemented ResourceLeaseBoundary.hpp (not wired to native callbacks). Local tests pass for three-frame delayed release, bounded capacity, rejection of rearming with active leases, counter-reset cancellation, owner-thread enforcement, and idempotent draining. Controller takes an already-valid owned control pair; it is not a resolver for captured raw addresses.

Integration inspection found SC6RecordEntry immediately returns if recorder active=false. recorder_stop also only sets active=false. Connecting delayed leases behind this gate would strand references on timeout/stop. Factory-to-boundary transfer cannot be implemented using stale raw pointers and has not been authorized by proof of current ownership. Installed DLL unchanged; no live holds performed. This controller alone does not complete live integration.

Added ResourceLeaseService.hpp and dispatch before SC6RecordEntry's active-recording gate, outside its SRW lock. recorder_stop queues cancellation rather than releasing on the polling/unload thread. Handler remains null: no delayed native lease acquisition or disposal enabled.

### Owned-reference cross-thread handoff

ResourceOwnedQueue reserves bounded capacity before atomically acquiring a strong reference from the currently factory-owned pair. Push never enqueues a borrowed pointer. The game thread pops and adopts that exact reference without a second increment, then holds for three subsequent frame advances. Queue close synchronizes with producers before cancellation drains; pre-clear and stop close acquisition. Maximum 16 total references per process. Live acquisition requires an observed boundary owner, but factory callback need not be on that thread. Native disposal remains on the owner thread.

Fixtures pass for worker-thread acquisition, unchanged count on adoption, three-frame release, cancellation of queued/unconsumed references, full/closed queue rejection, and one-use guard. Existing last-owner disposal fixtures remain applicable. Report uses total acquisitions minus releases to include queued references in pending, and separately reports transfers and queued pending. Factory-off-owner is now an observation, not rejection. Full build/live validation pending. Exit coverage remains limited to observed clear/frame paths; unexpected no-callback exit can retain bounded references until process termination. No rollback enabled.

### Snapshot dependency ownership storage

NativeSnapshotPool slots now retain up to eight explicit resource references using ResourceReferenceLease, requiring acquisition from a caller-owned validated pair. Duplicates, capacity overflow, stale handles, borrowed slots and foreign-thread operations reject without acquiring. Normal retirement drains dependencies before snapshot reconstruction; poisoned/faulted retirement also drains before preserving quarantine. Explicit release_dependencies invalidates/quarantines the slot so snapshots cannot be visited after resource ownership is discarded. Destruction releases any remaining dependencies only on the bound owner thread; incorrect-thread destruction with active references terminates rather than invoking native destructors on an arbitrary thread. Callers must explicitly drain on the simulation thread before teardown.

No live retention caller has been connected yet: resource dependency enumeration, native pair acquisition at capture, and transition teardown integration remain pending. This change provides ownership storage and retirement, not complete dependency coverage or continuous rollback. ### Generated query-buffer ownership and descriptor cross-pointers

Disassembled the complete chained body of 0x392080 in work/stage-generated-buffer-layout.txt (reproducer inspect_stage_generated_layout.py). Setup releases the previous root+0x430 pair via 0x2F79B0, obtains its replacement through allocator slot 0x78, copies the raw buffer to root+0x418/+0x428, and fills the root+8 table from that allocation cursor. The repeated relocation base therefore belongs to the separately reference-counted generated buffer, not the descriptor resource allocation. Existing allocator/control/destructor tracing identifies root+0x438 control, control vtable 0x3E89308, strong/weak counts +8/+0xC, buffer +0x18 and CRT-free callback 0x37C620.

The generated cell lists store pointers into descriptor records; root+0x408 also points into the descriptor. Setup rewrites encoded descriptor indices into pointers and marks records initialized. Retaining the generated buffer alone would not retain those referenced payloads; descriptor ownership alone does not retain the generated buffer. Neither this setup-time mutation nor earlier three-frame fingerprint equality proves permanent payload immutability.

Added exact pinned instruction assertions for buffer-to-table assignments and descriptor cross-pointer stores to audit_effect_lifetime.py, with explicit generated_handle_safe_acquisition_verified=false. ### Generated-buffer temporary owner acquisition candidate

Full allocator/caller disassembly archived in work/generated-owner-acquisition.txt, reproduced by work/inspect_generated_owner_acquisition.py. At 0x37C677 the allocator passes its local pair at RSP+0x28 to constructor 0x37C740. On return at 0x37C67C that pair holds a strong reference. The allocator increments control+8 at 0x37C687 before publishing control to destination+8 (0x37C699) and raw pointer to destination+0 (0x37C6A2), then writes the requested length as a QWORD at destination+0x10 (0x37C6E2). It releases its temporary strong reference at 0x37C6ED. This provides a synchronous constructor-return acquisition candidate before publication, conditional on successful construction and validated type/pointer/count. Root setup later consumes only the low signed DWORD of the length; future bounds checks must account for that distinction.

A constructor-return observer must filter the allocator call site and distinguish the destination stage pair from unrelated allocator users. A scoped setup/allocator context is preferable to assuming saved RSI survives a C++ hook wrapper unchanged. Construction occurs before query cells are initialized, so it is an ownership observation, not snapshot readiness or payload-stability proof. Thread identity and transfer to the snapshot owner remain unmeasured. Do not acquire from a later cached unowned control pointer. Added pinned assertions for call targets, temporary increment, publication, length and temporary release; executable audit passed. No runtime hook or reference mutation enabled, no DLL replacement, and no live test repeated.

### 2026-10-11: confirmations during scheduler stalls

Invalid identity is rejected at publication; scheduler conflict/expiry/invalid arrival is terminal. Full queue returns false so the producer must retry rather than mark delivery complete.

Added SchedulerInputSink with accepted/full/rejected outcomes and idempotent delivery tracking. It processes at most eight submissions per poll; a full queue leaves the record pending for retry, while rejection stops the diagnostic session. Observer queue saturation does not skip this independent submission loop. No sink is published by default.

### 2026-10-11: start-agreement wire exchange

Added an explicit 80-byte little-endian CombatStartPacket encoding (magic/version/kind, all offer fields and echoed ticket), plus CombatStartExchange. Receive requires mutual transport authentication, expected Steam sender and matching session nonce pair. Offers and ticket acknowledgements are idempotent before consumption; malformed size/version/kind/settings and mismatched senders reject. No padded native struct is transmitted.

SteamHandshake optionally handles these packets and retransmits the prepared exchange every 250 ms. The exchange pointer defaults null and is network-worker-owned once published; no arming flow publishes it yet. Packet size is distinct from existing 32-byte handshake and 96-byte input packets. NativeRollbackWindow tests passed for roundtrip encoding, two-peer acknowledgement exchange, wrong sender/no mutual proof, truncation/corruption and one-shot completion.

### 2026-10-11: bounded native start hold

Added NativeStartHold, bound to a verified clock identity/frame with a maximum ten-second deadline. It continues native service/wait checks but always withholds advancement. Timeout, changed frontier or native failure latches failure; no stock fallback. NativeClockHook can route a matching object through this optional hold before normal routing; pointer defaults null. An activated combat bundle takes precedence, and NativeCombatSession rejects activation through an expired/failed hold.
